2.31248pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}\kern 0.10919pt}\kern 1.29166pt\hbox{\kern 0.10919pt\hbox{\hbox{$\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\oc\Sigma^{\prime}\vdash N^{\prime}:\oc\tau^{\prime}$}\kern 0.0pt$}}\kern 0.10919pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}

Since
h(\mathcal{D}_{1}^{\prime})<h(\mathcal{D}_{1})
then
(h(\mathcal{D}_{1}^{\prime}),s(\oc\tau))<(h(\mathcal{D}_{1}),s(\oc\tau))
and by induction hypothesis we have a typing derivation
S(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})
of
\Gamma,\oc\Sigma^{\prime}\vdash M^{\prime}[N^{\prime}/z]:\sigma
. Moreover, since
s(\tau)<s(\oc\tau)
then
(h(S(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})),s(\tau))<(h(\mathcal{D}_{1}),s(\oc\tau))
, and by applying the induction hypothesis again there is a typing derivation
S(S(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2}),\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{2})
of
\Gamma,\Sigma^{\prime},\oc\Sigma^{\prime}\vdash M^{\prime}[N^{\prime}/z,N^{\prime}/y]:\sigma
. We conclude by applying a series of
\wn\mathsf{b}
rules and
\wn\mathsf{w}
rules.

Proposition 6.7

(Subject reduction)
.

Let
\mathcal{D}:\Gamma\vdash M:\sigma
. If
M\to_{\beta}N
then there is
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
such that
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}:\Gamma\vdash N:\sigma
.

Proof 6.8

(Proof)
.

It suffices to check that the reduction rules given in
Definition

6.1
preserve types. We consider the most interesting reduction rule, i.e.,
M=(\lambda x.P)Q\to_{\beta}P[Q/x]=N
. By inspecting the typing rules in
Figure

17
,
\mathcal{D}
must have the following structure:

\textstyle{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}_{1}}

\Sigma^{\prime}\vdash\lambda x.P^{\prime}:\tau\multimap B^{\prime}

\textstyle{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}_{2}}

\Delta^{\prime}\vdash Q^{\prime}:\tau

\scriptstyle{\multimap}_{e}\;

\Sigma^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}\vdash(\lambda x.P^{\prime})Q^{\prime}:B^{\prime}

\delta\ \vdots

\Sigma,\Delta\vdash(\lambda x.P)Q:\sigma

where:

•

\Gamma=\Sigma,\Delta
and
\sigma={{\oc\overset{n}{\ldots}\oc}}\forall\vec{X}.B
, for some
n\geq 0
,
\vec{X}
, and
B
.

•

\delta
is a sequence of rules in
\{\wn\mathsf{w},\wn\mathsf{b},\mathsf{f\mathsf{\oc p}},{\forall}_{i},{\forall}_{e}\}
,

•

B=B^{\prime}[\vec{C}/\vec{Y}]
, for some
\vec{C}
and
\vec{Y}
not free in
\Sigma^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}

•

P^{\prime}[\vec{x}/\vec{y}]=P
and
Q^{\prime}[\vec{x}/\vec{y}]=Q
, for some
\vec{x},\vec{y}
.

By a similar reasoning,
\mathcal{D}_{1}
has the following shape:

\textstyle{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}

\Sigma^{\prime\prime},x:\tau^{\prime}\vdash P^{\prime\prime}:B^{\prime\prime}

\scriptstyle{\multimap}_{i}\;

\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\vdash\lambda x.P^{\prime\prime}:\tau^{\prime}\multimap B^{\prime\prime}

\varepsilon\ \vdots

\Sigma^{\prime}\vdash\lambda x.P^{\prime}:\tau\multimap B^{\prime}

where:

•

\varepsilon
is sequences of typing rules in
\{\wn\mathsf{w},\wn\mathsf{b},{\forall}_{i},{\forall}_{e}\}
,

•

B^{\prime}=B^{\prime\prime}[\vec{D}/\vec{Z}]
and
\tau=\tau^{\prime}[\vec{D}/\vec{Z}]
, for some
\vec{D}
and
\vec{Z}
not free in
\Sigma^{\prime\prime}

•

P^{\prime\prime}[\vec{z}/\vec{w}]=P^{\prime}
for some
\vec{z},\vec{w}
.

Since
\tau=\tau^{\prime}[\vec{D}/\vec{Z}]
,
B^{\prime}=B^{\prime\prime}[\vec{D}/\vec{Z}]
and
\vec{Z}
do not occur free in
\Sigma^{\prime\prime}
, by
Lemma

6.4
there is a typing derivation
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
of
\Sigma^{\prime\prime},x:\tau\vdash P^{\prime\prime}:B^{\prime}
. By
Lemma

6.5
there is a typing derivation
S(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})
of
\Delta^{\prime},\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\vdash P^{\prime\prime}[Q^{\prime}/x]:B^{\prime}
. Finally, by applying the sequences of rules
\delta
and
\varepsilon
we obtain:

\textstyle{\scriptstyle S(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})}

\Delta^{\prime},\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\vdash P^{\prime\prime}[Q^{\prime}/x]:B^{\prime}

\vdots

\Delta^{\prime},\Sigma^{\prime}\vdash P^{\prime}[Q^{\prime}/x]:B^{\prime}

\vdots

\Sigma,\Delta\vdash P[Q/x]:\sigma

6.2.
Completeness results for
\mathsf{nuPTA}_{2\ell}
and
\mathsf{PTA}_{2\ell}

In this subsection we show completeness of
\mathsf{nuPTA}_{2\ell}
and
\mathsf{PTA}_{2\ell}
for, respectively,
\mathbf{FP}/\mathsf{poly}
and
\mathbf{FP}
. The proof adapts to our setting the encoding of polynomial time Turing machines from
[
MT03
,
GRDR09
]
.

6.2.1.
Definability and data types in
\mathsf{PTA}_{2\ell}
and
\mathsf{nuPTA}_{2\ell}

Polymorphic type systems based on linear logic typically encode inductive datatypes by universally quantified types (see, e.g.,
[
GRDR09
]
). Examples are natural numbers, defined by
\mathbf{N}\coloneqq\forall X.\oc(X\multimap X)\multimap X\multimap X
. Because of linearity restrictions on polymorphism, however, parsimonious logic cannot freely apply instantiation when encoding functions over inductive datatypes. As a consequence, its computational strength relative to standard notions of representability
[
Bar81
]
would be fairly poor. To circumvent this technical issue, following previous works on parsimonious logic
[
MT15
,
Maz15
]
, we adopt a parametric notion of representability, where natural numbers are defined by types of the form
\oc(A\multimap A)\multimap A\multimap A
, i.e., by instantiations of
\mathbf{N}
.

To this end, we generalise the usual notion of lambda definability
[
Bar81
]
to different kinds of input data:

Definition 6.9

(Representability
[
GRDR09
]
)
.

Let
f:\mathbb{I}_{1}\times\ldots\times\mathbb{I}_{n}\to\mathbb{O}
be a total function and let the elements
o\in\mathbb{O}
and
i_{j}\in\mathbb{I}_{j}
for
0\leq j\leq n
be encoded by terms
\underline{o}
and
\underline{i_{j}}
such that
\vdash\underline{o}:\mathbf{O}
and
\vdash\underline{i_{j}}:\mathbf{I}_{j}
. Then,
f
is
representable
in
\mathsf{nuPTA}_{2\ell}
(resp.
\mathsf{PTA}_{2\ell}
) if there is a term
\underline{f}\in\Lambda_{\mathsf{stream}}
such that
\vdash\underline{f}:\mathbf{I}_{1}\multimap\ldots\multimap\mathbf{I}_{n}\multimap\mathbf{O}
in
\mathsf{nuPTA}_{2\ell}
(resp.
\mathsf{PTA}_{2\ell}
) and

f\,i_{1}\ldots i_{n}=o\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\underline{f}\,\underline{i_{1}}\ldots\underline{i_{n}}\to_{\beta}^{*}\underline{o}

We adopt the usual notational convention
M^{n}\,N
(
n\geq 0
) defined inductively as
M^{0}(N)\coloneqq N
and
M^{n+1}(N)\coloneqq M(M^{n}(N))
. We also set
M\circ N\coloneqq\lambda z.M(Nz)
, which generalises to the
n
-ary case
M_{1}\circ\overset{}{\ldots}\circ M_{n}\coloneqq\lambda z.M_{1}(M_{2}(\ldots(M_{n}z)))
. Finally, the
n
-ary tensor product (with
n\geq 3
) can be defined from the binary one by setting

M_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes M_{n}\coloneqq(M_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes M_{n-1})\otimes M_{n}
,
\sigma_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes\sigma_{n}\coloneqq(\sigma_{1}\otimes\ldots\otimes\sigma_{n-1})\otimes\sigma_{n}
, and
\mathsf{let\ }{x_{1}\otimes\ldots}\otimes{x_{n}}={z}\mathsf{\ in\ }{M}\coloneqq\mathsf{let\ }{y}\otimes{x_{n}}={z}\mathsf{\ in\ }{(\mathsf{let\ }{x_{1}\otimes\ldots}\otimes{x_{n-1}}={y}\mathsf{\ in\ }{M})}
.
We also use the shorthand notation
\sigma^{n}\coloneqq\sigma\otimes\overset{n}{\ldots}\otimes\sigma
.

In what follows we encode some relevant data types and their basic operations in
\mathsf{nuPTA}_{2\ell}
(and
\mathsf{PTA}_{2\ell}
).

Definition 6.10

(Booleans)
.

Booleans
\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1}
and basic Boolean operations are encoded as in
Figure

18
.
The can be typed by
\mathbf{B}\coloneqq\forall X.(X\otimes X)\multimap(X\otimes X)
.

\begin{array}[]{rcrl}\underline{\mathbf{1}}&\coloneqq&\lambda x.\lambda y.x\otimes y&:\mathbf{B}\\
\underline{\mathbf{0}}&\coloneqq&\lambda x.\lambda y.y\otimes x&:\mathbf{B}\\
\mathbf{W_{\mathbf{B}}}&\coloneqq&\lambda b.\mathsf{let\ }{x_{1}}\otimes{x_{2}}={b(\mathsf{I}\otimes\mathsf{I})}\mathsf{\ in\ }{\mathsf{let\ }\mathsf{I}=\,{x_{2}}\mathsf{\ in\ }{x_{1}}}&:\mathbf{B}\multimap\mathbf{1}\\
\pi^{2}_{1}&\coloneqq&\lambda x.\mathsf{let\ }{x_{1}}\otimes{x_{2}}={x}\mathsf{\ in\ }{\mathsf{let\ }\mathsf{I}=\,{\mathbf{W_{\mathbf{B}}}\,x_{2}}\mathsf{\ in\ }{x_{1}}}&:\mathbf{B}\otimes\mathbf{B}\multimap\mathbf{B}\\
\mathbf{C_{\mathbf{B}}}&\coloneqq&\lambda b.\pi^{2}_{1}(b(\underline{\mathbf{1}}\otimes\underline{\mathbf{1}})\otimes(\underline{\mathbf{0}}\otimes\underline{\mathbf{0}}))&:\mathbf{B}\multimap\mathbf{B}\otimes\mathbf{B}\\
\underline{\neg}&\coloneqq&\lambda b.\lambda x.\lambda y.b(y\otimes x)&:\mathbf{B}\multimap\mathbf{B}\\
\underline{\vee}&\coloneqq&\lambda b_{1}.\lambda b_{2}.\pi_{1}^{2}(b_{1}\underline{\mathbf{0}}\,b_{2})&:\mathbf{B}\otimes\mathbf{B}\multimap\mathbf{B}\end{array}

Figure 18
.
Encoding of basic operations on Booleans.

The following is a straightforward consequence of the encodings in
Figure

18
.

Proposition 6.11

(Functional completeness)
.

Every Boolean function
f:\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1}\}^{n}\to\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1}\}^{m}
with
n\geq 0
,
m>0
can be represented by a term
\underline{f}\in\Lambda_{\mathsf{stream}}
such that
\vdash\underline{f}:\mathbf{B}^{n}\multimap\mathbf{B}^{m}
.

Notice that
Proposition

6.11
crucially relies on the terms
\mathbf{C_{\mathbf{B}}}
and
\mathbf{W_{\mathbf{B}}}
, which duplicate and erase Booleans in a purely linear fashion. Following
[
MT03
]
, we can generalise linear erasure of data to a fairly large class of types.

Definition 6.12

(
\Pi_{1}
and
e\Pi_{1}
types
[
MT03
]
)
.

Let
A
be a type build from
\mathbf{1},\otimes,\multimap,\forall
.
We say that
A
is
e\Pi_{1}
if every
\forall
-type occurring in it is inhabited.

Proposition 6.13

(Linear erasure
[
MT03
,
CR20
]
)
.

For any closed type
A
in
e\Pi_{1}
there is a term
\mathbf{W}_{A}
in a term that inhabits
A\multimap\mathbf{1}
.

Proposition 6.14

(Conditional)
.

For any
A
in
e\Pi_{1}
, the following rule is derivable:

\vdash R:A

\vdash L:A

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cond}\;

x:\mathbf{B}\vdash\mathsf{if}\,{x}\,\mathsf{then\,}{R}\,\mathsf{else}\,{L}:A

where
\mathsf{if}\,{x}\,\mathsf{then\,}{R}\,\mathsf{else}\,{L}
satisfies the following reductions:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathsf{if}\,{\underline{\mathbf{1}}}\,\mathsf{then\,}{R}\,\mathsf{else}\,{L}&\to_{\beta}^{*}&R\\
\mathsf{if}\,{\underline{\mathbf{0}}}\,\mathsf{then\,}{R}\,\mathsf{else}\,{L}&\to_{\beta}^{*}&L\end{array}

Proof 6.15
.

We set
\mathsf{if}\,{x}\,\mathsf{then\,}{R}\,\mathsf{else}\,{L}\coloneqq\pi^{2}_{1}(xRL)
, where
\pi^{2}_{1}
is as in
Figure

18
.

Definition 6.16

(Streams of Booleans)
.

A stream (of Booleans)
\alpha
is encoded by a term
\mathbf{M}
such that
\mathbf{M}(i)\coloneqq\underline{\alpha(i)}
. We write
\underline{\alpha}
for the encoding of
\alpha
.
Streams can be typed by
\mathbf{Stream}\coloneqq\omega{\mathbf{B}}
.

Definition 6.17

(Natural numbers and Boolean strings)
.

The encoding of Boolean strings and natural numbers is as follows, for any
n\geq 0
and
s=b_{1}\cdots b_{n}\in\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1}\}^{*}
:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\underline{n}&\coloneqq&\lambda f.\lambda z.f^{n}z\\
\underline{s}&\coloneqq&\lambda f.\lambda z.f\,\underline{b_{n}}(f\,\underline{b_{n-1}}(\ldots(f\,\underline{b_{1}}\,z)\ldots))\end{array}

For any type
A
, natural numbers and Boolean strings can be typed, respectively, by

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathbf{N}[A]&\coloneqq&\oc(A\multimap A)\multimap A\multimap A\\
\mathbf{S}[A]&\coloneqq&\oc(\mathbf{B}\multimap A\multimap A)\multimap A\multimap A\end{array}

With
\mathbf{N}[]
we denote
\mathbf{N}[A]
for some
A
, and similarly for
\mathbf{S}[]
.

We need to encode the function that, when applied to a Boolean string, returns its length:

Proposition 6.18

(Length)
.

There exists a term
\mathsf{length}
of type
\mathbf{S}[A]\multimap\mathbf{N}[A]
satisfying the following reduction, for all
s=b_{1}\cdots b_{n}\in\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1}\}^{*}
:

\mathsf{length}\,\underline{s}\to_{\beta}^{*}\underline{n}

Proof 6.19
.

We set
\mathsf{length}\coloneqq\lambda s.\lambda f.s(\lambda x.\lambda y.\mathsf{let\ }\mathsf{I}=\,{\!\!\mathbf{W_{\mathbf{B}}}\,x}\mathsf{\ in\ }{fy})
, where
\mathbf{W_{\mathbf{B}}}
is as in
Figure

18
.

The following proposition shows that encodings of natural numbers and Boolean strings can be used as iterators.

Proposition 6.20

(Iteration)
.

For any
A
, the following rule is derivable

\oc\Gamma\vdash S:\oc(A\multimap A)

\Delta\vdash B:A

\scriptstyle\mathsf{iter}_{\mathbf{N}}\;

\oc\Gamma,\Delta,n:\mathbf{N}[A]\vdash\mathsf{iter}_{\mathbf{N}}\,{n}\,{S}\,{B}:A

where
\mathsf{iter}_{\mathbf{N}}\,{n}\,{S}\,{B}
satisfies the reduction

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathsf{iter}_{\mathbf{N}}\,{\underline{n}}\,{S}\,{B}&\to_{\beta}^{*}&S^{n}B\end{array}

Similarly, the following rule is derivable:

\oc\Gamma\vdash S_{0}:\oc(A\multimap A)

\oc\Gamma\vdash S_{1}:\oc(A\multimap A)

\Delta\vdash B:A

\scriptstyle\mathsf{iter}_{\mathbf{S}}\;

\oc\oc\Gamma,\Delta,n:\mathbf{S}[A]\vdash\mathsf{iter}_{\mathbf{S}}\,{n}\,{S_{0}}\,{S_{1}}:A

where
\mathsf{iter}_{\mathbf{S}}\,{n}\,{S_{0}}\,{S_{1}}
satisfies the reduction

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathsf{iter}_{\mathbf{N}}\,{\underline{b_{1}\cdots b_{n}}}\,{S_{0}}\,{S_{1}}B&\to_{\beta}^{*}&S_{b_{n}}\ldots S_{b_{1}}B\end{array}

Proof 6.21
.

It suffices to set, respectively,
\mathsf{iter}_{\mathbf{N}}\,{\coloneqq}\,{\lambda}\,{n}.\lambda s.\lambda b.nsb
and
\mathsf{iter}_{\mathbf{S}}\,{\coloneqq}\,{\lambda}\,{s}\lambda t.\lambda u.\lambda b.stub
.

Our next goal is to show that any polynomial over natural numbers can be encoded in
\mathsf{nuPTA}_{2\ell}
(and
\mathsf{PTA}_{2\ell}
). The encoding of polynomials requires nesting types, so we introduce a notation for denoting iterated nesting in a succinct way.

Definition 6.22

(Nesting)
.

Let
A
be a type. We define
\mathbf{N}_{A}[d]
and
\mathbf{S}_{A}[d]
by induction on
d\geq 0
:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathbf{N}_{A}[0]&\coloneqq&A\\
\mathbf{N}_{A}[{d+1}]&\coloneqq&\mathbf{N}_{A}[\mathbf{N}_{A}[d]]\end{array}\qquad\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathbf{S}_{A}[0]&\coloneqq&A\\
\mathbf{S}_{A}[{d+1}]&\coloneqq&\mathbf{S}_{A}[\mathbf{S}_{A}[d]]\end{array}

If
A
is clear from the context, we simply write
\mathbf{N}[d]
and
\mathbf{S}[d]
.

Proposition 6.23
.

For any
d\geq 0
, there exist a term
\mathsf{down}^{d}_{\mathbf{N}}
of type
\mathbf{N}[{d+1}]\multimap\mathbf{N}[{d}]
and a term
\mathsf{down}^{d}_{\mathbf{S}}
of type
\mathbf{S}[{d+1}]\multimap\mathbf{S}[{d}]

satisfying the following reductions:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathsf{down}^{d}_{\mathbf{N}}\,\underline{n}&\to_{\beta}^{*}&\underline{n}\\
\mathsf{down}^{d}_{\mathbf{S}}\,\underline{n}&\to_{\beta}^{*}&\underline{n}\end{array}

Proof 6.24
.

We set
\mathsf{down}^{d}_{\mathbf{N}}\coloneqq\lambda x.\mathsf{iter}_{\mathbf{N}}\,{x}\,{\mathsf{succ}}\,{\underline{0}}
and
\mathsf{down}^{d}_{\mathbf{S}}\coloneqq\lambda x.\mathsf{iter}_{\mathbf{N}}\,{x}\,{(}\,{\lambda}b.\lambda s.\lambda c.\lambda z.cb(scz))\underline{\epsilon}
.

Definition 6.25

(Successor, addition, multiplication)
.

Successor, addition and multiplication can be represented by the following terms:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathsf{succ}&\coloneqq&\lambda n.\lambda f.\lambda z.n(f)(fz)&\\
\mathsf{add}&\coloneqq&\lambda n.\lambda m.\mathsf{iter}_{\mathbf{N}}\,{n}\,{(}\,{\mathsf{succ}})\,m\\
\mathsf{mult}&\coloneqq&\lambda n.\lambda m.\mathsf{iter}_{\mathbf{N}}\,{m}\,{(\lambda y.\mathsf{add}\,n\,y)}\,{\underline{0}}&\end{array}

they are typable as follows:

\begin{array}[]{rl}&\vdash\mathsf{succ}:\mathbf{N}[i]\multimap\mathbf{N}[i]\\
&\vdash\mathsf{add}:\mathbf{N}[i+1]\multimap\mathbf{N}[i]\multimap\mathbf{N}[i]\\
&\vdash\mathsf{mult}:\oc\mathbf{N}[i+1]\multimap\mathbf{N}[i+1]\multimap\mathbf{N}[i]\end{array}

Theorem 6.26

(Polynomial completeness)
.

Let
p(x):\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}
be a polynomial with degree
\delta(p)>0
. Then there is a term
\underline{p}
representing
p
such that, for any
i\geq 0
:

x:\oc^{\delta(p)-1}\mathbf{N}[\delta(p)+i]\vdash\underline{p}:\mathbf{N}[i]

Proof 6.27
.

For the sake of readability, we will avoid writing the index
i
in typing judgements. Thus, with
\mathbf{N}[n]
we mean
\mathbf{N}[n+i]
.

Consider a polynomial
p(x):\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}
in Horner normal form, i.e.,
p(x)=a_{0}+x(a_{1}+x(\ldots(a_{n-1}+xa_{n})\ldots))
. We actually show something stronger:

x_{0}:\mathbf{N}[1],x_{1}:\oc\mathbf{N}[2],\ldots,x_{n-1}:\oc^{n-1}\mathbf{N}[n]\vdash\underline{\hat{p}}:\mathbf{N}[0]

(7)

where
\hat{p}=a_{0}+x_{0}(a_{1}+x_{1}(\ldots(a_{n-1}+x_{n-1}a_{n})\ldots))
. The proof is by induction on
\delta({p})=n
. If
\delta({p})=1
then
\hat{p}=a_{0}+x_{0}a_{1}
, and we simply set
\underline{\hat{p}}\coloneqq\mathsf{add}\,\underline{a_{0}}\,{(\mathsf{mult}\,\underline{a_{1}}\,x_{0})}
. If
\delta({p})>1
then
\hat{p}=a_{0}+x_{0}\hat{q}
with
\hat{q}\coloneqq a_{1}+x_{1}(a_{2}+x_{2}(\ldots(a_{n-1}+x_{n-1}a_{n})\ldots))
. By induction hypothesis on
q
we have

x_{1}:\mathbf{N}[1],x_{2}:\oc\mathbf{N}[2],\ldots,x_{n-1}:\oc^{n-2}\mathbf{N}[n-1]\vdash\underline{\hat{q}}:\mathbf{N}[0]

By repeatedly applying
\mathsf{down}^{k}_{\mathbf{N}}
for appropriate
k
we obtain a term
M
such that:

x_{1}:\mathbf{N}[2],x_{2}:\oc\mathbf{N}[3],\ldots,x_{n-1}:\oc^{n-2}\mathbf{N}[n]\vdash M:\mathbf{N}[0]

We set
\underline{\hat{p}}\coloneqq\mathsf{add}\,\underline{a_{0}}\,(\mathsf{mult}\,M\,x_{0})
, which is typable as:

x_{0}:\mathbf{N}[1],x_{1}:\oc\mathbf{N}[2],\ldots,x_{n-1}:\oc^{n-1}\mathbf{N}[n]\vdash\underline{\hat{p}}:\mathbf{N}[0]

and we can conclude since
\delta({q})=\delta({p})-1
.

Now, to prove the theorem it suffices to repeatedly apply
\mathsf{down}^{k}_{\mathbf{N}}
for appropriate
k
to the typable term in
Equation

7
in order to get a term
N
that represents
\hat{p}
and typable as

x_{0}:\mathbf{N}[n],x_{1}:\oc\mathbf{N}[n],\ldots,x_{n-1}:\oc^{n-1}\mathbf{N}[n]\vdash\underline{\hat{p}}:\mathbf{N}[0]

By applying a series of
\wn\mathsf{b}
we obtain a term
\underline{p}
representing the polynomial
p
and such that
x:\oc^{\delta(p)-1}\mathbf{N}[\delta(p)]\vdash\underline{p}:\mathbf{N}[0]
.

6.2.2.
Encoding polytime Turing machines with polynomial advice in
\mathsf{nuPTA}_{2\ell}

In this subsection we show how to encode a Turing machine working in polynomial time with access to advice in
\mathsf{nuPTA}_{2\ell}
, following essentially
[
MT03
,
GRDR09
]
.

W.l.o.g., we will assume that the alphabet of the machine is composed by the two symbols
\mathbf{1}
and
\mathbf{0}
9
9

9

We can encode the alphabet
\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1},\sqcup\}
within the alphabet
\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1}\}
, by setting
\sqcup\coloneqq\mathbf{0}\mathbf{0}
,
\mathbf{0}\coloneqq\mathbf{0}\mathbf{1}
and
\mathbf{1}\coloneqq\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}
.
, and that final states are divided into accepting and rejecting.

A
configuration

C
of the machine will be represented by a term of the following form:

\underline{C}\coloneqq\lambda c.(c\underline{b^{l}_{0}}\circ\ldots\circ c\underline{b^{l}_{n}})\otimes(c\underline{b^{r}_{0}}\circ\ldots\circ c\underline{b^{r}_{m}})\otimes\underline{q}\otimes\underline{\alpha}

(8)

where:

•

b^{r}_{0}\in\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1}\}
is the scanned symbol

•

b^{r}_{1}\cdots b^{r}_{m}\in\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1}\}
are the symbols of the tape to the right of the scanned symbol

•

b^{l}_{n}\cdots b^{l}_{0}\in\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1}\}
are the symbols of the tape to the left of the scanned symbol (notice that we encode this tuple in reverse order)

•

q=b_{1}\cdots b_{k}\in\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1}\}
is the (encoding of the) current state of the machine.

•

\alpha
represents the advice of the machine as a single Boolean stream (see
Proposition

2.6
).

Terms as in
Equation

8
have the following type:

\mathbf{TM}\coloneqq\forall X.\oc(\mathbf{B}\multimap X\multimap X)\multimap((X\multimap X)^{2}\otimes\mathbf{B}^{k}\otimes\mathbf{Stream})

where
\mathbf{Stream}
is as in
Definition

6.16
.

The
initial configuration

C_{0}
describes a machine with tape filled by blank symbols (here
\mathbf{0}\mathbf{0}
s) the head at the beginning of the tape and in the initial state
q_{0}
. To render the construction of the initial configuration in
\mathsf{nuPTA}_{2\ell}
, we define the following term:

\mathsf{init}\coloneqq\lambda n.\lambda c.(\lambda z.z)\otimes n(\lambda z^{\prime}.c\underline{\mathbf{0}}(c\underline{\mathbf{0}}z^{\prime}))\otimes\underline{q_{0}}\otimes\underline{\alpha}

(9)

It takes the encoding of a natural number
n
in input and returns the term

\underline{C_{0}}\coloneqq\lambda c.(\lambda z.z)\otimes(c\underline{\mathbf{0}}\circ\overset{2n}{\ldots}\circ c\underline{\mathbf{0}})\otimes\underline{q}\otimes\underline{\alpha}

representing the first
n
blank symbols of the tape. Terms as in
Equation

9
have the type below

\mathbf{N}[d]\multimap\mathbf{Stream}\multimap\mathbf{TM}

for all
d\geq 0
.

Following
[
MT03
,
GRDR09
]
, in order to show that Turing machine transitions are representable we consider two distinct phases:

•

A
decomposition phase
, where the encoding of the configuration
C
is decomposed to extract the symbols
\underline{b^{l}_{0}}
,
\underline{b^{r}_{0}}
.

•

A
composition phase
, where the components of
C
are assembled back to get the configuration of the machine after the transition.

The decomposition of a configuration has type
\mathbf{ID}
:

\mathbf{ID}\coloneqq\forall X.\oc(\mathbf{B}\multimap A[X])\multimap(A[X]^{2}\otimes B[X]^{2}\otimes\mathbf{B}^{k}\otimes\mathbf{Stream})

where
A[X]\coloneqq X\multimap X
and
B[X]\coloneqq(\mathbf{B}\multimap A[X])\otimes\mathbf{B}
.

The decomposition phase is described by the term
\mathsf{dec}
of type
\mathbf{TM}\multimap\mathbf{ID}
defined as follows:

\mathsf{dec}\coloneqq\lambda m.\lambda c.\mathsf{let\ }{l}\otimes{r\otimes q\otimes\alpha}={m\,(F[c])}\mathsf{\ in\ }{}\\
(\mathsf{let\ }{s_{l}}\otimes{c_{l}\otimes b^{l}_{0}}={l(\mathsf{I}\otimes(\lambda x.\mathsf{let\ }\mathsf{I}=\,{\mathbf{W}_{\mathbf{B}}\,x}\mathsf{\ in\ }{\mathsf{I}})\otimes\underline{\mathbf{0}})}\mathsf{\ in\ }{}\\
(\mathsf{let\ }{s_{r}}\otimes{c_{r}\otimes b^{r}_{0}}={r(\mathsf{I}\otimes(\lambda x.\mathsf{let\ }\mathsf{I}=\,{\mathbf{W}_{\mathbf{B}}\,x}\mathsf{\ in\ }{\mathsf{I}})\otimes\underline{\mathbf{0}})}\mathsf{\ in\ }{}\\
s_{l}\otimes s_{r}\otimes c_{l}\otimes\otimes b^{l}_{0}\otimes c_{r}\otimes b^{r}_{0}\otimes q\otimes\underline{\alpha}))

(10)

where
\mathbf{W}_{\mathbf{B}}
is the eraser for
\mathbf{B}
given by
Proposition

6.13
, and
F[x]\coloneqq\lambda b.\lambda z.\mathsf{let\ }{g}\otimes{h\otimes i}={z}\mathsf{\ in\ }{(h\,i\circ g)\otimes x\otimes b}
, which is typable as:

x:\mathbf{B}\multimap A[X]\vdash F[x]:B[(A[X]\otimes B[X])/X]

The term
\mathsf{dec}
in
Equation

10
satisfies the reduction in
Figure

19
.

\begin{array}[]{c}\mathsf{dec}\,(\lambda c.(c\underline{b^{l}_{0}}\circ\ldots\circ c\underline{b^{l}_{n}})\otimes(c\underline{b^{r}_{0}}\circ\ldots\circ c\underline{b^{r}_{m}})\otimes\underline{q}\otimes\underline{\alpha})\\[8.5359pt]
\to_{\beta}^{*}\\[8.5359pt]
\lambda c.(cb^{l}_{1}\circ\ldots\circ cb^{l}_{n})\otimes(cb^{r}_{1}\circ\ldots\circ cb^{r}_{m})\otimes c\otimes b^{l}_{0}\otimes c\otimes b^{r}_{0}\otimes\underline{q}\otimes\underline{\alpha}\\[17.22217pt]
\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{c}\mathsf{comp}\ (\lambda c.(cb^{l}_{1}\circ\ldots\circ cb^{l}_{n})\otimes(cb^{r}_{1}\circ\ldots\circ cb^{r}_{m})\otimes c\otimes b^{l}_{0}\otimes c\otimes b^{r}_{0}\otimes\underline{q}\otimes\underline{\alpha})\\[8.5359pt]
\to_{\beta}^{*}\\[8.5359pt]
\begin{cases}\lambda c.(cb^{\prime}\circ cb^{l}_{0}\circ\ldots\circ cb^{l}_{n})\otimes(cb^{r}_{1}\circ\ldots\circ cb^{r}_{m})\otimes\underline{q^{\prime}}\otimes\underline{{tl}{(\alpha)}}&\text{if }\delta(b^{r}_{0},{hd}{(\alpha)},q)=(b^{\prime},q^{\prime},\text{Right})\\[4.30554pt]
\lambda c.(cb^{l}_{1}\circ\ldots\circ cb^{l}_{n})\otimes(cb^{l}_{0}\circ cb^{\prime}\circ cb^{r}_{1}\circ\ldots\circ cb^{r}_{m})\otimes q^{\prime}\otimes\underline{{tl}{(\alpha)}}&\text{if }\delta(b^{r}_{0},{hd}{(\alpha)},q)=(b^{\prime},q^{\prime},\text{Left})\end{cases}\end{array}

Figure 19
.
Reductions for
\mathsf{dec}
and
\mathsf{comp}
.

Analogously, the composition phase is described by the term
\mathsf{comp}
of type
\mathbf{ID}\multimap\mathbf{TM}
defined as follows:

\mathsf{comp}\coloneqq\lambda s.\lambda c.\mathsf{let\ }{l}\otimes{r\otimes c_{l}\otimes b_{l}\otimes c_{r}\otimes b_{r}\otimes q\otimes\alpha}={s\,c}\mathsf{\ in\ }{}\\
\mathsf{let\ }{h}\otimes{t}={\mathsf{pop}\,\alpha}\mathsf{\ in\ }{}(\mathsf{let\ }{b^{\prime}}\otimes{q^{\prime}\otimes m}={\underline{\delta}(b_{r}\otimes h\otimes q)}\mathsf{\ in\ }{}\\
((\mathsf{if}\,{m}\,\mathsf{then\,}{R}\,\mathsf{else}\,{L})b^{\prime}q^{\prime}(l\otimes r\otimes c_{l}\otimes b_{l}\otimes c_{r})\otimes t)

(11)

where
\mathsf{if}\,{m}\,\mathsf{then\,}{R}\,\mathsf{else}\,{L}
is defined as in
Proposition

6.14
, and

\begin{array}[]{rcl}R&\coloneqq&\lambda b^{\prime}.\lambda q^{\prime}.\lambda s.\mathsf{\,let}\,l\otimes r\otimes c_{l}\otimes b_{l}\otimes c_{r}=s\mathsf{\,in\,}(c_{r}b^{\prime}\circ c_{l}b_{l}\circ l)\otimes r\otimes q^{\prime}\\[8.61108pt]
L&\coloneqq&\lambda b^{\prime}.\lambda q^{\prime}.\lambda s.\,\mathsf{let\,}l\otimes r\otimes c_{l}\otimes b_{l}\otimes c_{r}=s\mathsf{\,in\,}l\otimes(c_{l}b_{l}\circ c_{r}b^{\prime}\circ r)\otimes q^{\prime}\\
\end{array}

The term
\mathsf{comp}
in
Equation

11
satisfies the reduction in
Figure

19
, where
\delta:\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1}\}^{k+2}\to\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1}\}^{k+3}
is the transition function of the Turing machine, which takes as an extra input the first bit of the current advice stack, i.e., the head of the stream
\alpha
.

Remark 6.28
.

Notice that in
\mathsf{comp}
the variable
m
has type
\mathbf{B}
and is applied to the terms
R
and
L
. This requires to apply to the variable
m
the rule
{\forall}_{e}
, which instantiates the type variable
X
with the
\oc
-free type
\mathbf{B}\multimap\mathbf{B}^{k}\multimap((X\multimap X)^{2}\otimes(\mathbf{B}\multimap X\multimap X)\otimes\mathbf{B}\otimes(\mathbf{B}\multimap X\multimap X))\multimap(X\multimap X)^{2}\otimes\mathbf{B}^{k}
.

By combining the above terms we obtain the encoding of the Turing machine transition step:

\mathsf{Tr}\coloneqq\mathsf{comp}\circ\mathsf{dec}

(12)

with type
\mathbf{TM}\multimap\mathbf{TM}
.

We now need a term that encodes the
initialisation
of the machine with an input Boolean string. This is given by the term
\mathsf{In}

of type
\mathbf{S}[\mathbf{TM}]\multimap\mathbf{TM}\multimap\mathbf{TM}
defined as follows:

\mathsf{In}\coloneqq\lambda s.\lambda m.s(\lambda b.(Tb)\circ\mathsf{dec})\,m

(13)

where
\mathsf{dec}
is defined as in
Equation

10
and

\begin{array}[]{rcl}T&\coloneqq&\lambda b.\lambda s.\lambda c.\,\mathsf{let\ }{l}\otimes{r\otimes c_{l}\otimes b_{l}\otimes c_{r}\otimes b_{r}\otimes q\otimes\alpha}={sc}\mathsf{\ in\ }\\
&&(\mathsf{let\ }\mathsf{I}=\,{\mathbf{W}_{\mathbf{B}}\,b_{r}}\mathsf{\ in\ }{(Rbq(l\otimes r\otimes c_{l}\otimes b_{l}\otimes c_{r}))}\otimes\alpha)\\[8.61108pt]
R&\coloneqq&\lambda b^{\prime}.\lambda q^{\prime}.\lambda s.\,\mathsf{let\ }{l}\otimes{r\otimes c_{l}\otimes b_{l}\otimes c_{r}}={s}\mathsf{\ in\ }{}((c_{r}b^{\prime}\circ c_{l}b_{l}\circ l)\otimes r\otimes q^{\prime})\end{array}

Intuitively, the term
\mathsf{In}
defines a function that, when supplied with a Boolean string
s
and a Turing machine
M
, writes
s
as input on the tape of
M
.

Finally, we need a term that
extracts
the output string from the final configuration. This is given by the term
\mathsf{Ext}
of type
\mathbf{TM}\multimap\mathbf{S}
, defined as follows:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\mathsf{Ext}&\coloneqq&\lambda s.\lambda c.\,\mathsf{let\ }{l}\otimes{r\otimes q\otimes\alpha}={sc}\mathsf{\ in\ }{}({\mathsf{let\ }\mathsf{I}=\,{\mathbf{W}_{\mathbf{B}^{k+1}}\,(q\otimes(\mathsf{disc}\,{\alpha}))}\mathsf{\ in\ }{l\circ r})}\end{array}

(14)

where
\mathsf{disc}
is the eraser for streams (see
Figure

17
) and
\mathbf{W}_{\mathbf{B}^{k+1}}
is the eraser for
\mathbf{B}^{k+1}
given by
Proposition

6.13
.

We can now prove our fundamental theorem:

Theorem 6.29
.

Let
f:(\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1}\}^{*})^{n}\to\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1}\}^{*}
:

(1)

If
f\in\mathbf{FP}/\mathsf{poly}
then
f
is representable in
\mathsf{nuPTA}_{2\ell}
;

(2)

If
f\in\mathbf{FP}
then
f
is representable in
\mathsf{PTA}_{2\ell}
.

Proof 6.30
.

We only show the case where
f
is a unary function for the sake of simplicity.
Let us prove
Item

1
. If
f\in\mathbf{FP}/\mathsf{poly}
then,
f\in\mathbf{FP}(\mathbb{R})
by
Proposition

2.6
, so there is a polynomial Turing machine computing
f
that performs polynomially many queries to bits of a Boolean stream
\alpha
. Let
p(x)
and
q(x)
be polynomials bounding, respectively, the time and space of the Turing machine, and let
\delta(p)=m
and
\delta(q)=l
be their degrees. By
Theorem

6.26
we obtain
\underline{p}
and
\underline{q}
typable as:

\begin{array}[]{l}y:\oc^{m-1}\mathbf{N}[m+1]\vdash\underline{p}:\mathbf{N}[1]\\
z:\oc^{l-1}\mathbf{N}[l+1]\vdash\underline{q}:\mathbf{N}[1]\end{array}

where
\mathbf{N}[i]
is shorthand notation for
\mathbf{N}_{\mathbf{TM}}[i]
(see
Definition

6.22
). By applying
Lemma

6.5
and
Proposition

6.18
, we have:

\begin{array}[]{l}s^{\prime}:\oc^{m-1}\mathbf{S}[m+1]\vdash P:\mathbf{N}[1]\\
s^{\prime\prime}:\oc^{l-1}\mathbf{S}[l+1]\vdash Q:\mathbf{N}[1]\end{array}

where
P\coloneqq\underline{p}[\mathsf{length}\,s^{\prime}/y]:
and
Q\coloneqq\underline{q}[\mathsf{length}\,s^{\prime\prime}/z]
. On the other hand, by applying again
Lemma

6.5
and
Equations

9
,
13
,
12
and
14
:

t:\mathbf{S}[1],p:\mathbf{N}[1],q:\mathbf{N}[1]\vdash\mathsf{Ext}((p\,\mathsf{Tr})(\mathsf{In}\,t\,(\mathsf{init}\,q\,\alpha))):\mathbf{S}

By putting everything together we have:

s^{\prime}:\oc^{m-1}\mathbf{S}[m+1],s^{\prime\prime}:\oc^{l-1}\mathbf{S}[l+1],t:\mathbf{S}[1]\vdash N:\mathbf{S}

where
N\coloneqq\mathsf{Ext}((P\,\mathsf{Tr})(\mathsf{In}\,t\,(\mathsf{init}\,Q\,\alpha)))
.
By repeatedly applying
\wn\mathsf{b}
and
\mathsf{down}^{k}_{\mathbf{S}}
for appropriate
k
we obtain a term
M
representing
f
such that:

s:\oc^{\max(m,l)}\mathbf{S}[\max(m,l)+1]\vdash M:\mathbf{S}

By applying
\mathsf{down}^{1}_{\mathbf{S}}
we obtain

x:\mathbf{S}[\oc^{\max(m,l)}\mathbf{S}[\max(m,l)+1]]\vdash M[\mathsf{down}^{1}_{\mathbf{S}}\,x/s]:\mathbf{S}

We set
\underline{f}\coloneqq M[\mathsf{down}^{1}_{\mathbf{S}}\,x/s]
, so that
x:\mathbf{S}[]\vdash\underline{f}:\mathbf{S}
.

Item

2
follows directly from
Item

1
by stripping away streams from the above encoding.

6.3.
Translations and completeness theorem

We can compare the computational strength of type systems and inductive proof systems based on parsimonious linear logic by means of a translation.

Definition 6.31

(Translation)
.

We define a translation
(\_)^{\dagger}
from
\mathsf{nuPTA}_{2\ell}
to
\mathsf{nuPLL}_{2\ell}
mapping typing derivations of
\mathsf{nuPTA}_{2\ell}
to derivations of
\mathsf{nuPLL}_{2\ell}
such that, when restricted to typing derivations of
\mathsf{PTA}_{2\ell}
, it returns derivations of
\mathsf{PLL}_{2\ell}
:

•

It maps types of
\mathsf{nuPTA}_{2\ell}
to formulas of
\mathsf{nuPLL}_{2\ell}
according to the following inductive definition:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}X^{\dagger}&\coloneqq&X\\
\mathbf{1}^{\dagger}&\coloneqq&\mathbf{1}\\
(\sigma\multimap A)^{\dagger}&\coloneqq&\sigma^{\dagger}\multimap A^{\dagger}\\
(\forall X.A)^{\dagger}&\coloneqq&\forall X.A^{\dagger}\\
(\sigma\otimes\tau)^{\dagger}&\coloneqq&\sigma^{\dagger}\otimes\tau^{\dagger}\\
(\oc\sigma)^{\dagger}&\coloneqq&\oc\sigma^{\dagger}\\
(\omega{\sigma})^{\dagger}&\coloneqq&\oc{\sigma^{\dagger}}\end{array}

we notice that
\sigma^{\dagger}[\tau^{\dagger}/X]=(\sigma[\tau/X])^{\dagger}
.

•

It maps a context
\Gamma=x_{1}:\sigma_{1},\ldots,x_{n}:\sigma_{n}
to a sequent
\Gamma^{\dagger}=\sigma_{1}^{\dagger},\ldots,\sigma_{n}^{\dagger}
.

•

It maps judgements
\Gamma\vdash M:\tau
to sequents
{\Gamma^{\dagger}}^{\bot},\tau^{\dagger}
.

•

It maps a typing rule to gadgets as in
Figure

20
and
Figure

21
.

and

, respectively.

Lemma 6.32
.

For any
\mathcal{D}_{1}:\Gamma\vdash M:\sigma
and
\mathcal{D}_{2}:\Delta\vdash N:\tau
there is
S(\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})
such that:

\small{{}{}\vbox{\hbox{\kern 10.83pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\left({{{}{}{}}\vbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\kern 6.07336pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern Proof 6.33
.

It suffices to check that the derivation
S(\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})
can be stepwise computed by the cut elimination rules.

Lemma 6.34
.

Let
\mathcal{D}_{1}:\Gamma\vdash M_{1}:\sigma
. If
M_{1}\to_{\beta}M_{2}
then there is a typing derivation
\mathcal{D}_{2}:\Gamma\vdash M_{2}:\sigma
such that
\mathcal{D}_{1}^{\dagger}\to_{\mathsf{cut}}^{*}\mathcal{D}_{2}^{\dagger}
.

Proof 6.35
.

It suffices to check the statement for the reduction rules in
Definition

6.1
, by inspecting the cut elimination rules of
\mathsf{nuPLL}_{2\ell}
. We consider the two most relevant cases. If
M_{1}=\mathsf{pop}\,\mathbf{M}
and
M_{2}={hd}{(\mathbf{M})}\otimes{tl}{(\mathbf{M})}
, then w.l.o.g.
\mathcal{D}_{1}
has the following shape:

\scriptstyle\mathsf{pop}\;

\vdash\mathsf{pop}:\omega{\sigma}\multimap\sigma\otimes\omega{\sigma}

\vdash\mathbf{M}(0):\sigma

\vdash\mathbf{M}(1):\sigma

\ldots

\vdash\mathbf{M}(n):\sigma

\ldots

\scriptstyle\mathsf{stream}\;

\vdash\mathbf{M}:\omega{\sigma}

\scriptstyle{\multimap}_{e}\;

\vdash\mathsf{pop}\,\mathbf{M}:\omega{\sigma}\otimes\omega{\sigma}

We set
\mathcal{D}_{2}
as the following typing derivation:

\vdots

\vdash\mathbf{M}(0):\sigma

\vdash\mathbf{M}(1):\sigma

\vdash\mathbf{M}(2):\sigma

\ldots

\vdash\mathbf{M}(n+1):\sigma

\ldots

\scriptstyle\mathsf{stream}\;

\vdash tl(\mathbf{M}):\omega{\sigma}

\scriptstyle\otimes\;

\vdash\mathbf{M}(0)\otimes tl(\mathbf{M}):\sigma\otimes\omega{\sigma}

It is easy to check that
\mathcal{D}_{1}^{\dagger}\to_{\mathsf{cut}}^{*}\mathcal{D}_{2}^{\dagger}
.

Let
M_{1}=(\lambda x.P)N
and
M_{2}=P[N/x]
. By inspecting the typing rules in
Figure

17

\mathcal{D}
must have the following structure:

\textstyle{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}_{1}}

\Sigma^{\prime}\vdash\lambda x.P^{\prime}:\tau\multimap B^{\prime}

\textstyle{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}_{2}}

\Delta^{\prime}\vdash Q^{\prime}:\tau

\scriptstyle{\multimap}_{e}\;

\Sigma^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}\vdash(\lambda x.P^{\prime})Q^{\prime}:B^{\prime}

\delta\ \vdots

\Sigma,\Delta\vdash(\lambda x.P)Q:\sigma

where:

•

\Gamma=\Sigma,\Delta
and
\sigma={{\oc\overset{n}{\ldots}\oc}}\forall\vec{X}.B
, for some
n\geq 0
,
\vec{X}
, and
B
.

•

\delta
is a sequence of rules in
\{\wn\mathsf{w},\wn\mathsf{b},\mathsf{f\mathsf{\oc p}},{\forall}_{i},{\forall}_{e}\}
,

•

B=B^{\prime}[\vec{C}/\vec{Y}]
, for some
\vec{C}
and
\vec{Y}
not free in
\Sigma^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime}

•

P^{\prime}[\vec{x}/\vec{y}]=P
and
Q^{\prime}[\vec{x}/\vec{y}]=Q
, for some
\vec{x},\vec{y}
.

By a similar reasoning,
\mathcal{D}_{1}
has the following shape:

\textstyle{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1}}

\Sigma^{\prime\prime},x:\tau^{\prime}\vdash P^{\prime\prime}:B^{\prime\prime}

\scriptstyle{\multimap}_{i}\;

\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\vdash\lambda x.P^{\prime\prime}:\tau^{\prime}\multimap B^{\prime\prime}

\varepsilon\ \vdots

\Sigma^{\prime}\vdash\lambda x.P^{\prime}:\tau\multimap B^{\prime}

where:

•

\varepsilon
is sequences of typing rules in
\{\wn\mathsf{w},\wn\mathsf{b},{\forall}_{i},{\forall}_{e}\}
,

•

B^{\prime}=B^{\prime\prime}[\vec{D}/\vec{Z}]
and
\tau=\tau^{\prime}[\vec{D}/\vec{Z}]
, for some
\vec{D}
and
\vec{Z}
not free in
\Sigma^{\prime\prime}

•

P^{\prime\prime}[\vec{z}/\vec{w}]=P
for some
\vec{z},\vec{w}
.

Since
\tau=\tau^{\prime}[\vec{D}/\vec{Z}]
,
B^{\prime}=B^{\prime\prime}[\vec{D}/\vec{Z}]
and
\vec{Z}
do not occur free in
\Sigma^{\prime\prime}
, by
Lemma

6.4
there is a typing derivation
\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1}
of
\Sigma^{\prime\prime},x:\tau\vdash P^{\prime\prime}:B^{\prime}
. By
Lemma

6.5
there is a typing derivation
S(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})
of
\Delta^{\prime},\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\vdash P^{\prime\prime}[Q^{\prime}/x]:B^{\prime}
. Finally, by applying the sequences of rules
\delta
and
\varepsilon
we obtain:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\small\widehat{\mathcal{D}}&\coloneqq&{{{}{}{}}\vbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ $}}\kern 10.00002pt}\hbox{\hbox{$\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ $}}\kern 10.00002pt}\hbox{\hbox{$\ \ \ \ \ \ \ $}}}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$$}}\vbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 75.00002pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-17.33066pt\raise 20.47462pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.0228pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle S(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})}$}}}}}}}}}}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$$}\hss}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 75.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{$$}}\kern 75.00002pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 25.92235pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$$}}\vbox{}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$$}\hss}\kern 25.92235pt}\hbox{\kern 25.92235pt\hbox{\hbox{$\Delta^{\prime},\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\vdash P^{\prime\prime}[Q^{\prime}/x]:B^{\prime}$}}\kern 25.92235pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 25.92235pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to98.15535pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}\kern 25.92235pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 75.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{$\kern 5.0pt\hbox{$\vdots$}\kern 5.0pt$}}\kern 75.00002pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 28.2279pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to93.54424pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}\kern 28.2279pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 28.2279pt\hbox{\hbox{$\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\Delta^{\prime},\Sigma^{\prime}\vdash P^{\prime}[Q^{\prime}/x]:B^{\prime}$}\kern 0.0pt$}}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}\kern 28.2279pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 75.00002pt\hbox{\hbox{$\kern 5.0pt\hbox{$\vdots$}\kern 5.0pt$}}\kern 75.00002pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 33.92819pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to82.14365pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}\kern 33.92819pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 33.92819pt\hbox{\hbox{$\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\Sigma,\Delta\vdash P[Q/x]:\sigma$}\kern 0.0pt$}}\kern 33.92819pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}\end{array}

Let us now show that
\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}\to_{\mathsf{cut}}^{*}\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{\dagger}
. First, notice that
\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}
is as follows:

\textstyle{\scriptstyle(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}_{1})^{\dagger}}

{(\Sigma^{\prime\prime})^{\dagger}}^{\bot},{(\tau^{\prime})^{\dagger}}^{\bot},(B^{\prime\prime})^{\dagger}

\scriptstyle\invamp\;

{(\Sigma^{\prime\prime})^{\dagger}}^{\bot},{(\tau^{\prime})^{\dagger}}^{\bot}\invamp(B^{\prime\prime})^{\dagger}

\varepsilon^{\dagger}\ \vdots

{(\Sigma^{\prime})^{\dagger}}^{\bot},{\tau^{\dagger}}^{\bot}\invamp(B^{\prime})^{\dagger}

\textstyle{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}_{2}^{\dagger}}

{(\Delta^{\prime})^{\dagger}}^{\bot},{\tau^{\dagger}},

\scriptstyle\mathsf{ax}\;

{(B^{\prime})^{\dagger}}^{\bot},(B^{\prime})^{\dagger}

\scriptstyle\otimes\;

{(\Delta^{\prime})^{\dagger}}^{\bot},\tau^{\dagger}\otimes{(B^{\prime})^{\dagger}}^{\bot},(B^{\prime})^{\dagger}

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}\;

{(\Sigma^{\prime})^{\dagger}}^{\bot},{(\Delta^{\prime})^{\dagger}}^{\bot},(B^{\prime})^{\dagger}

\delta^{\dagger}\,\vdots

{\Sigma^{\dagger}}^{\bot},{\Delta^{\dagger}}^{\bot},\sigma^{\dagger}

Moreover, since
\tau=\tau^{\prime}[\vec{D}/\vec{Z}]
,
B^{\prime}=B^{\prime\prime}[\vec{D}/\vec{Z}]
and
\vec{Z}
do not occur free in
\Sigma^{\prime\prime}
, the above derivation reduces by cut elimination to the following:

\textstyle{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}_{2}^{\dagger}}

{(\Delta^{\prime})^{\dagger}}^{\bot},{\tau^{\dagger}},

\textstyle{\scriptstyle(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1})^{\dagger}}

{(\Sigma^{\prime\prime})^{\dagger}}^{\bot},{\tau^{\dagger}}^{\bot},(B^{\prime})^{\dagger}

\scriptstyle\mathsf{cut}\;

{(\Delta^{\prime})^{\dagger}}^{\bot},{(\Sigma^{\prime\prime})^{\dagger}}^{\bot},(B^{\prime})^{\dagger}

\vdots

{(\Delta^{\prime})^{\dagger}}^{\bot},{(\Sigma^{\prime})^{\dagger}}^{\bot},(B^{\prime})^{\dagger}

\vdots

{\Delta^{\dagger}}^{\bot},{\Sigma^{\dagger}}^{\bot},\sigma^{\dagger}

for some
\varepsilon^{\dagger}
and
\delta^{\dagger}
. By
Lemma

6.32
the above derivation reduces by cut elimination to the following:

\left({{{}{}{}}\vbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\ \ \ \ \ \ $}}\kern 9.24994pt}\hbox{\hbox{$\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ $}}\kern 9.24994pt}\hbox{\hbox{$\ \ \ \ \ \ $}}}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$$}}\vbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 60.1246pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-15.32379pt\raise 32.82742pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.78435pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle S(\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2})}$}}}}}}}}}}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$$}\hss}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 1.29166pt\hbox{\kern 60.1246pt\hbox{\hbox{$$}}\kern 60.1246pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 14.09691pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$$}}\vbox{}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$$}\hss}\kern 14.09691pt}\hbox{\kern 14.09691pt\hbox{\hbox{$\Delta^{\prime},\Sigma^{\prime\prime}\vdash P^{\prime\prime}[Q^{\prime}/x]:B^{\prime}$}}\kern 14.09691pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}\right)^{\dagger}

\vdots

{(\Delta^{\prime})^{\dagger}}^{\bot},{(\Sigma^{\prime})^{\dagger}}^{\bot},(B^{\prime})^{\dagger}

\vdots

{\Delta^{\dagger}}^{\bot},{\Sigma^{\dagger}}^{\bot},\sigma^{\dagger}

which is
\widehat{\mathcal{D}}^{\dagger}
.

Theorem 6.36
.

Let
f:(\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1}\}^{*})^{n}\to\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1}\}^{*}
:

(1)

If
f
is representable in
\mathsf{PTA}_{2\ell}
then it is in
\mathsf{PLL}_{2\ell}
;

(2)

If
f
is representable in
\mathsf{nuPTA}_{2\ell}
then so it is in
\mathsf{nuPLL}_{2\ell}
.

Proof 6.37
.

We only consider the case where
f
is unary for the sake of simplicity. Let
\underline{f}
be a typable term of
\mathsf{nuPTA}_{2\ell}
representing
f
, so that
\underline{f}\,\underline{s}\to_{\beta}^{*}\underline{f(s)}
for any
s\in\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1}\}^{*}
. Consider the following derivation:

\mathcal{D}={{{{}{}{}}}{{{}{}{}}}\vbox{\hbox{\kern 13.82945pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\kern to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$$}}\vbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 27.50002pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt}}}\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 1.6667pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$$}}\vbox{}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$$}\hss}\kern 1.6667pt}\hbox{\kern to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$$}}\vbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 27.50002pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise-1.89168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\mathcal{D}_{s}}$}}}}}}}}}}$}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$$}\hss}\kern 0.0pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 27.50002pt\hbox{\hbox{$$}}\kern 27.50002pt}}}\kern 14.58334pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vdash\underline{s}:\mathbf{S}[]$}}\kern 14.58334pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 1.6667pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\multimap}_{e}\;$}}$}}\vbox{\vbox to0.4pt{\vfill\hbox to103.75003pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}\kern 14.58334pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 37.29172pt\hbox{\hbox{$\vdash\underline{f}\,\underline{s}:\mathbf{S}[]$}}\kern 50.20836pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}

By repeatedly applying
Lemma

6.34
there is
\mathcal{D}_{f(s)}
such that

\mathcal{D}^{\dagger}={{{{}{}{}}}{{{{}{}{}}}{}}\vbox{\hbox{\kern 3.4221pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 27.50002pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}\kern 0.0pt\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 8.61116pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$$}}\vbox{}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$$}\hss}\kern 8.61116pt}\hbox{\kern to144.97635pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}\kern 0.0pt}\vfill}}\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\smash{\lower 2.5pt\hbox{$$}}$}\hss}\kern 14.19861pt}\kern 1.43518pt\hbox{\kern 75.12712pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathbf{S}[]$}}\kern 80.71457pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}\quad\to_{\mathsf{cut}}^{*}\quad{{{}{}{}}\vbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{$\ \ $}}\kern 10.00002pt}\hbox{\hbox{$\ \ \ \ \ \ \ $}}\kern 10.00002pt}\hbox{\hbox{$\ \ $}}}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\hbox{$$}}\vbox{\hbox{$\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 37.50002pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 31.5278pt\hbox{\hbox{$\mathbf{S}[]$}}\kern 31.5278pt}}}\kern 0.0pt}}}

in
\mathsf{nuPLL}_{2\ell}
, where we can safely assume that
\mathcal{D}_{s}^{\dagger}\to_{\mathsf{cut}}^{*}\underline{s}
and
\mathcal{D}_{f(s)}^{\dagger}\to_{\mathsf{cut}}^{*}\underline{f(s)}
in
\mathsf{nuPLL}_{2\ell}
. This means that
\mathcal{D}_{f}^{\dagger}
represents
f
in
\mathsf{nuPLL}_{2\ell}
. If moreover
\underline{f}
is typable term of
\mathsf{PTA}_{2\ell}
then
\mathcal{D}_{f}^{\dagger}
represents
f
in
\mathsf{PLL}_{2\ell}
.

Theorem 6.38

(Completeness)
.

Let
f:(\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1}\}^{*})^{n}\to\{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{1}\}^{*}
:

(1)

If
f\in\mathbf{FP}/\mathsf{poly}
then
f
is representable in
\mathsf{wr}\mathsf{PLL}_{2\ell}^{\infty}
;

(2)

If
f\in\mathbf{FP}
then
f
is representable in
\mathsf{r}\mathsf{PLL}_{2\ell}^{\infty}
.

Proof 6.39
.

For
i\in\{1,2\}
, Item (i) follows from
Theorem

6.29
.(i),
Theorem

6.36
.(i) and
Theorem

4.39
.(i).

We conclude the section by discussing some computational aspects of the finiteness condition on the typing rule
\mathsf{ib}\mathsf{\oc p}
, and the restriction on second-order instantiation to
(\oc,\omega)
-free types in
\mathsf{nuPTA}_{2\ell}
.

Remark 6.40
.

If the side condition on the typing rule
\mathsf{stream}
(i.e., that
\{\mathbf{M}(i)\mid i\in\mathbb{N}\}
is finite) were dropped, then
\mathsf{nuPTA}_{2\ell}
would represent any function on natural numbers. Indeed, given a function
f:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}
, we can define the term
\mathbf{F}\coloneqq\underline{f(0)}::\underline{f(1)}::\ldots
with type
\omega{\oc\mathbf{N}}
, encoding all the values of the function
f
. We set
A\coloneqq\mathbf{N}[\mathbf{1}]\otimes\omega{\mathbf{N}}[\mathbf{1}]
and define:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathsf{step}&\coloneqq&\lambda x.\mathsf{let\ }{y_{1}}\otimes{y_{2}}={x}\mathsf{\ in\ }{\mathsf{let\ }\mathsf{I}=\,{y_{1}\,(\lambda z.z)\,\mathsf{I}}\mathsf{\ in\ }{\mathsf{pop}\,y_{2}}}\\
\underline{f}&\coloneqq&\lambda n.\mathsf{let\,}x\otimes y=\!(n\,\mathsf{step}\,(\mathsf{pop}\ \mathbf{F}))\mathsf{\,in\,}\mathsf{let\ }\mathsf{I}=\,{\!\!(\mathsf{disc}\,y)}\mathsf{\ in\ }{x}\end{array}

where
\mathsf{step}
has type
A\multimap A
and
\underline{f}
has type
\mathbf{N}[A]\multimap\mathbf{N}[\mathbf{1}]
. It is easy to check that
\underline{f}\,\underline{n}\to_{\beta}^{*}\underline{f(n)}
, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

This observation can be easily adapted to the proof systems
\mathsf{nuPLL}_{2\ell}
(w.r.t. the finiteness condition on
\mathsf{ib}\mathsf{\oc p}
) and
\mathsf{wr}\mathsf{PLL}_{2\ell}^{\infty}
(w.r.t. the weak regularity condition).

Remark 6.41
.

If the
(\oc,\omega)
-freeness condition on
{\forall}_{e}
were dropped then
\mathsf{nuPTA}_{2\ell}
could represent exponential functions. Indeed, we can define the following functions:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathsf{plustwo}&\coloneqq&\lambda n.\lambda f.\lambda z.nf(f(fz))&:\mathbf{N}[]\multimap\mathbf{N}[]\\
\mathsf{double}&\coloneqq&\lambda n.n\,(\mathsf{plustwo})\,\underline{0}&:\mathbf{N}[\mathbf{N}[]]\multimap\mathbf{N}[]\\
\mathsf{exp}&\coloneqq&\lambda n.n\ (\mathsf{double})\ \underline{1}&:\mathbf{N}[\mathbf{N}[]]\multimap\mathbf{N}[]\end{array}

It is easy to check that, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
:

\mathsf{plustwo}\ \underline{n}\to_{\beta}^{*}\underline{n+2}\qquad\mathsf{double}\ \underline{n}\to_{\beta}^{*}\underline{2n}\qquad\mathsf{exp}\ \underline{n}\to_{\beta}^{*}\underline{2^{n}}

This observation can be easily adapted to the proof systems
\mathsf{nuPLL}_{2\ell}
and
\mathsf{wr}\mathsf{PLL}_{2\ell}^{\infty}
(w.r.t. the
\oc
-freeness condition of instantiations in the rule
\exists
).

7.
Conclusion and future work

This paper builds on a series of recent works aimed at developing implicit computational complexity in the setting of cyclic and non-wellfounded proof theory
[
CD22
,
CD23
]
. We proved that the non-wellfounded proof systems
\mathsf{wr}\mathsf{PLL}_{2\ell}^{\infty}
and
\mathsf{r}\mathsf{PLL}_{2\ell}^{\infty}

capture the complexity classes
\mathbf{FP}/\mathsf{poly}
and
\mathbf{FP}
respectively.
We then establish a series of characterisations for various finitary proof systems.

We envisage extending the contribution of this paper, among others, to the following research directions.

Polynomial time over the reals

[
HMP20
]
introduces a characterisation of Ko’s class of polynomial time computable functions over real numbers
[
Ko91
]
based on parsimonious logic.
By employing the co-absorption rule
\oc\mathsf{b}
to represent the
pop
operation on streams, this complexity class could be modelled within
\mathsf{PLL}_{2\ell}^{\infty}
via cut elimination as in
[
ACG24
]
.

Probabilistic complexity

De-randomisation methods showing the inclusion of the complexity class
\mathbf{BPP}
(bounded-error probabilistic polynomial time) in
\mathbf{FP}/\mathsf{poly}
suggest that this class can be characterised within
\mathsf{wr}\mathsf{PLL}_{2\ell}^{\infty}
. Challenges are expected, since
\mathbf{BPP}
is defined by explicit (error) bounds, as observed in
[
LT15
]
(so, not entirely in the style of ICC), but we conjecture that error bounds can be traded for appropriate global proof-theoretic conditions on
\mathsf{wr}\mathsf{PLL}_{2\ell}^{\infty}
that restrict computationally the access to streams.

Logarithmic Space

In
[
MT15
,
Maz15
]
the authors characterize the complexity classes
\mathbf{L}
(logarithmic space problems) and its non-uniform counterpart
\mathbf{L}/\mathsf{poly}
(problems decided by polynomial size branching programs) by stripping away second-order quantifiers from their proof systems capturing
\mathbf{P}
and
\mathbf{P}/\mathsf{poly}
.
We expect that a similar result can be obtained for our non-wellfounded proof systems.

Non-uniform Proofs-as-Processes

Processes such as a scheduler sorting tasks among a (finite) set of servers according to a predetermined order (e.g., a token ring of servers) may easily be modelled by
nwb
s,
making
\mathsf{wr}\mathsf{PLL}_{2\ell}^{\infty}
appealing for the study of the proofs-as-processes correspondence and its applications
[
Abr94
,
CP10
,
Wad14
,
DGS17
,
MP21
]
.
